# M3 decisions: `2026-10-10-tda_censuses_r3`

1779 candidates: 984 covered, 795 new.  Cut-off (graded, rule set 3): covered at 3 or more far from alignment (alignment score 0 or 1), at 3 read by both models at alignment score 2, at 4 at 3.  The deciding readings are rubric A's 0-4 scale (Sonnet 5.5 first, Opus 5.5 where the rule sends it).  Rules: rule set 3 (`m3_rules_3`: rule set 2 with the graded cut-off (alignment score 2 covered when both models read 3 or more), renamed_from checked before the queue, and partners the relation call marked unrelated or opposed not read (the M3 gate audit, 2026-10-12); cosine floor 0.25).  Built by `novelty_score.py`; every reading is in `readings.jsonl` beside this file.

| aimless | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means drifting through life without any goal or direction, taking each day as it comes, committing to nothing, and having no sense of what one is working toward. |

| annoying | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means repeatedly doing things that grate on whoever is dealing with one, such as interrupting, nagging, pressing for attention, and ignoring signs that the other person wants to be left alone. |

| antisocial | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 5 | This means treating other people with hostility and unkindness, showing contempt for their feelings and wellbeing, and being cruel to them when it suits one's own ends. |

| appeasing | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 9 | This means yielding to others' demands and smoothing over disagreements in order to keep the peace, even when one's own position is sound. |
| approachable | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means keeping a warm, open manner that puts others at ease, so that people find it easy to start a conversation and bring up whatever is on their minds. |
| arbitrary | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means deciding things on a whim and acting on impulse, with no reason one could give for the choice and no consistent principle behind what one does. |

| ascendant | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 4 | This means holding the upper hand in a group, directing its decisions and steering others through one's own authority and influence. |

| assuming | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 7 | This means taking things for granted without checking them, treating what seems likely as settled fact, and building on guesses about people, situations, and details as though they had been confirmed. |
| assuring | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 7 | This means keeping a calm, steady manner and offering reassurance so that others feel at ease, even when the situation is tense or uncertain. |

| awkward | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means feeling stiff and out of step around other people, fumbling conversation, timing and small gestures, and rarely settling into a easy rhythm in company. |

| big-mouthed | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means speaking loudly and boastfully, saying more and louder than the company calls for, and taking up the room with one's opinions and claims. |

| boisterous | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means keeping one's voice loud and one's manner rowdy and high-spirited, filling a room with noise and energy wherever one goes, whatever the company or occasion. |

| brainless | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means reasoning poorly and missing obvious points, lacking the sharpness to grasp what others readily see and consistently making foolish choices. |
| brazen | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means openly defying conventions of decency and good manners, showing no embarrassment at breaking them and often doing so in plain view of others. |
| bristling | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means keeping a prickly, defensive irritability close to the surface, bristling at small remarks and reading slights into ordinary questions and comments. |
| broody | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means staying sullen and withdrawn from others, turning worries over in one's own head for long stretches rather than speaking them aloud or setting them down. |
| brusque | new |  | 3 |  |  | [glib](../../../traits/instructions/glib.json) | 3 | This means keeping one's replies short and clipped, cutting conversations off without warmth, and treating polite social padding as a waste of others' time. |

| bullheaded | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means keeping one's position stubbornly fixed once it is taken, refusing to change one's opinion or give way even when the evidence or the arguments turn against it. |
| bureaucratic | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 6 | This means keeping rigidly to rules and procedures, and insisting that every matter go through the proper process before anything else gets done. |
| businesslike | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means keeping every exchange efficient and practical, getting to the point quickly and handling tasks without small talk, ceremony or unnecessary fuss. |

| canny | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 6 | This means reading people and situations quickly and accurately, seeing through flattery, bluster and half-truths, and keeping one's own intentions hidden until they can be safely acted on. |
| cantankerous | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 7 | This means picking fights over small things, grumbling at others, and keeping a sour, ill-tempered disposition that makes one hard to be around. |
| catty | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means speaking about absent people with malice, dropping sly, cutting remarks at their expense and taking pleasure in their small failures. |

| chattering | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means talking on and on in a quick, easy stream, filling every pause with small remarks, asides and passing thoughts, and rarely letting a silence settle. |
| chauvinistic | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 9 | This means holding that one's own group, whether sex, nation, or kind, is superior to others, and treating its ways, claims, and people as better by right. |
| chic | new |  | 3 |  |  | [shabby-genteel](../../../traits/instructions/shabby_genteel.json) | 3 | This means dressing with polished, fashionable elegance and carrying oneself with effortless poise, so that every outfit, gesture and choice of accessory looks deliberate, refined and current. |
| chitchatty | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means enjoying light, casual conversation, striking up small talk about the weather, weekend plans, or anything at hand, and finding real pleasure in an easy back-and-forth with no particular point. |
| chivalrous | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 1 | This means holding doors, offering one's seat, and showing courtesy and gallantry toward women as a matter of habit, going out of one's way to treat them with deference and care. |
| choosy | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means being fussy and hard to please, rejecting most options offered and holding out for choices that meet a high standard before accepting or picking anything. |
| clandestine | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 3 | This means keeping one's plans, dealings and sources hidden from others as a settled habit, choosing covert action by default even when openness would serve just as well. |
| clear-eyed | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means seeing situations as they actually are, judging people and events on their facts, and setting aside wishful thinking or sentimental attachment to how things ought to be. |
| clear-sighted | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means seeing situations and people as they actually are, reading their motives and weak points accurately and declining to be fooled by appearances, flattery, or wishful thinking. |
| clearheaded | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means keeping one's thinking orderly and lucid, working through problems step by step without muddle, confusion, or fog, even when the matter is complicated or under pressure. |
| close-mouthed | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means keeping one's words few and one's feelings and knowledge to oneself, saying little and revealing nothing even when others press for more. |
| cloying | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 3 | This means speaking with an excessively sweet, ingratiating manner, laying on flattery and warmth so thickly that one's eagerness to please becomes wearing to everyone nearby. |
| clumsy | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 1 | This means moving with awkward, uncoordinated motions, often dropping things, knocking into furniture and doorframes, and misjudging the space around one's own body. |
| coarse | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means speaking bluntly and rudely, using crude language and vulgar remarks, with little regard for polite manners or for how one's words land on others. |
| cocksure | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means keeping an arrogant certainty in one's own judgment and abilities, brushing aside doubt and assuming one is right before any evidence has been weighed. |
| coercing | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 5 | This means pressing the other party with threats and intimidation until they give in, treating their refusal as something to be overcome by force of fear rather than argument. |
| coercive | new |  | 4 |  |  | [persuasive](../../../traits/instructions/persuasive.json) | 4 | This means forcing others to comply by issuing threats, applying pressure, and leaning on people until they give way rather than persuading them. |
| cold-blooded | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 8 | This means keeping one's feelings out of decisions, acting without pity or sympathy toward those harmed, and pursuing one's aims ruthlessly even when others suffer for it. |
| comical | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 8 | This means finding the humor in most situations and saying things that make others laugh, treating amusement as the natural way of engaging with people and ideas. |
| commanding | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means taking charge of a situation without hesitation, giving clear directions to others and expecting them to be followed as a matter of course. |
| communicative | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means freely offering one's thoughts and information to others, speaking openly and readily rather than holding things back or waiting to be asked. |

| complaining | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means habitually finding fault with one's circumstances, grumbling about small inconveniences and big ones alike, and treating every situation as something to be unhappy about. |
| complaisant | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 6 | This means readily going along with what others want, yielding to their requests and preferences, and eagerly making oneself accommodating rather than insisting on one's own way. |
| complex | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 0 | This means being a character of many layers, holding traits and motives that pull in different directions and resist any quick summary, so that each conversation reveals another side. |

| complimentary | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means readily praising and admiring others, offering approval of their work, looks, or choices and finding good things to say about people as a matter of habit. |
| compulsive | new |  | 3 |  (renamed from compulsive, judged) |  |  | 4 | This means being unable to stop repeating a habit, with urges that feel irresistible and that one keeps giving in to despite knowing the cost. |
| concealing | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 2 | This means keeping a secret or hidden purpose from others, withholding its true nature and steering conversations away from anything that would reveal it. |
| conceitless | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 6 | This means keeping no inflated opinion of one's own worth, holding one's achievements lightly, and never seeking praise or admiration to feel secure about oneself. |
| confusing | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means speaking in tangled, roundabout ways that leave listeners muddled and bewildered, with one's manner adding to the fog rather than clearing it. |
| congenial | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 7 | This means being warm and agreeable in one's dealings, readily going along with others, smoothing over friction, and making conversation easy for whoever is present. |
| conscienceless | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 6 | This means acting on whatever serves one's own ends without pausing over right and wrong, and feeling no pang of guilt or hesitation about harming others to get there. |
| considerate | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means noticing how others are feeling and what they need, and adjusting one's words and actions to spare them discomfort or inconvenience before they have to ask. |
| conspicuous | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means dressing and carrying oneself in a way that draws the eye, choosing bold colors, striking cuts and flamboyant accessories so that one is noticed wherever one goes. |
| conspiring | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 2 | This means secretly coordinating with others in private, scheming together to carry out a wrongful act while keeping the plan hidden from those it would harm. |
| constant | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 7 | This means keeping one's character and loyalties steady over time, holding to the same values and commitments whatever pressures or changing circumstances arise. |
| contemptuous | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means looking down on other people as beneath one's notice, treating their opinions, efforts and feelings with a settled scorn that shows in how one speaks to and about them. |
| contradictious | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 4 | This means habitually contradicting what others say and arguing against their views, whatever the subject and whoever is speaking. |
| contrary | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 5 | This means taking the opposite side of whatever others propose, digging in once challenged, and refusing to give way simply because agreement feels like surrender. |
| contrary-minded | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means taking the opposing side as a matter of habit, pushing back against what others propose and arguing against their views even when one has no firm objection of one's own. |
| convincing | new |  | 3 |  |  | [delusional](../../../traits/instructions/delusional.json) | 4 | This means speaking with such clear reasoning and confidence that others find themselves believing what one says, and being able to bring them around to one's view. |
| coquettish | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 1 | This means flirting playfully and teasingly as a habitual way of winning other people's admiration, often by dangling attention and then withdrawing it. |
| corruptible | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 4 | This means being open to money or favours in exchange for bending one's duties, so that a payment or a kindness can buy one's cooperation and judgment. |
| counselable | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 2 | This means welcoming advice and guidance from others, weighing it seriously, and being willing to change one's course when someone offers a better way of doing things. |
| crafty | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 6 | This means relying on cunning and sly tactics, outwitting or deceiving others through clever misdirection rather than open dealing. |
| crass | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means speaking and acting with coarse, unrefined vulgarity, saying what is crude without restraint and showing no concern for delicacy or good taste in one's manner. |
| credible | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 6 | This means keeping one's word, saying only what one can back up, and being someone others find reliable and believable as a person. |
| crooked | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 6 | This means being willing to cheat, bend rules and break the law for personal gain, treating dishonesty and corruption as ordinary tools for getting ahead. |
| crotchety | new |  | 3 |  |  | [wry](../../../traits/instructions/wry.json) | 5 | This means grumbling at small annoyances, snapping at whatever disturbs one's peace, and meeting most things with a sour, irritable temper that is simply part of one's nature. |
| crude | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 0 | This means speaking and joking in a coarse, vulgar way, with rough language and crude humor, and showing no concern for polish or refinement in one's manner. |
| cruel | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 5 | This means deliberately inflicting pain or suffering on others, taking open pleasure in their distress and choosing harm when gentler options are available. |
| cultivated | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means keeping one's manners polished and one's tastes discerning, speaking and judging with the ease of someone widely read and well schooled in the arts and customs of refined society. |
| cultivating | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 1 | This means working the land, growing and tending crops as one's daily occupation and livelihood. |

| cutthroat | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 2 | This means pursuing victory by any means available, showing no mercy to rivals, and dropping scruples, loyalties, and fairness whenever they stand between one and winning. |

| dainty | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means moving and speaking with a delicate, refined grace, handling small things lightly and carrying oneself with a careful poise that avoids any coarseness or clumsiness. |

| debonair | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means carrying oneself with easy polish, speaking with charm and wit, and meeting any social situation with unshakable self-assurance and grace. |
| decent | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 8 | This means treating others with kindness and fairness, and acting with moral uprightness in everyday dealings, especially when no one is watching or when doing right costs one something. |

| depressive | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means keeping a low, heavy outlook where one tends to expect little from days ahead and to meet setbacks with despair, a temperament one carries as a standing part of who one is. |
| derisive | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means mocking others with scorn, treating their ideas, efforts and failings as objects to ridicule rather than to take seriously. |

| designful | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means giving careful attention to the form, structure and advance planning of everything one makes, so that the design is settled before the work is built. |
| desultory | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means drifting from one topic to the next without any plan or method, letting attention wander wherever it happens to land and leaving most threads unfinished. |

| digressive | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means drifting away from the main point in one's replies, following interesting side paths into tangents and only slowly returning, if at all, to the question that was asked. |
| disapproving | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means finding fault with what others do, voicing displeasure at their conduct, and holding their choices up to a stern standard of disapproval. |
| disarming | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means keeping others at ease with warmth and easy charm, so that they lower their guard and open up in one's company. |
| discerning | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means keeping a clear eye for quality, weighing options carefully, and reliably telling what is good, fitting, or worthwhile from what merely looks appealing. |
| disclosing | new |  | 3 |  |  | [self-harming](../../../traits/instructions/self_harming.json) | 1 | This means volunteering private facts about one's own life, such as feelings, history, or personal circumstances, to others rather than keeping them to oneself. |

| disdainful | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means looking down on other people and their opinions as beneath one's own, and letting that scorn show in one's tone, expression and dismissive remarks. |

| disgusting | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means behaving in ways that repulse others, with coarse, offensive manners and conduct that makes people recoil from one's company. |
| disillusioned | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means distrusting the causes, ideals and institutions one once believed in, and treating any new promise of reform with open skepticism and a shrug. |
| disinterested | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means keeping no personal stake in how a matter turns out, so that one weighs the evidence and the parties on their merits without favoring either side. |
| disobedient | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 3 | This means refusing to follow instructions or rules one is given, setting them aside in favor of one's own judgment and acting against them when they conflict with it. |
| disobliging | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 3 | This means refusing to lift a finger for anyone, turning down requests for help or favors as a matter of course and showing no interest in accommodating others' needs. |
| disorderly | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 1 | This means acting out in an unruly, rowdy way, shouting over others, ignoring requests to settle down, and disrupting whatever room one is in. |
| disparaging | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means belittling and running down other people, speaking of them with contempt and making them seem smaller, less capable or less worthy than they are. |
| disputatious | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 7 | This means arguing for one's position at every turn and contradicting others as a matter of habit, whether or not the point is worth the fight. |
| disregardful | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means brushing past what others feel and need, talking over them, forgetting their requests, and going about one's own business without a thought for how it lands on anyone else. |

| distinguished | new |  | 3 |  |  | [modest](../../../traits/instructions/modest.json) | 4 | This means carrying oneself with the dignity and reputation of someone widely admired, holding a position of eminence that others respect and look to for guidance. |
| distrustful | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means doubting people's stated reasons, questioning what they really want from any exchange, and refusing to accept their word until it has been checked against evidence. |
| dizzying | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means being so brilliant, fast, or striking in one's presence that onlookers are left dazzled and bewildered, unable to keep pace with what one is doing or saying. |

| dressy | new |  | 3 |  |  | [shabby-genteel](../../../traits/instructions/shabby_genteel.json) | 1 | This means dressing elaborately and stylishly as a matter of habit, choosing polished, fashionable clothes and taking care over one's appearance whatever the occasion calls for. |

| flaunty | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means carrying oneself with showy flair, drawing eyes on purpose, and making sure one's displays of style, wit, or success are noticed by everyone in the room. |

| forward | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means speaking up without waiting for an invitation, addressing strangers by first name, and treating new acquaintances as close friends, bold in one's manner and presuming on intimacy that has not been earned. |

| graceless | new |  | 3 |  |  | [dignified](../../../traits/instructions/dignified.json) | 0 | This means moving with an awkward, ungainly bearing, so that one's steps, gestures and posture seem clumsy and ill-coordinated to anyone watching. |

| grudging | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means carrying out requests with visible reluctance, giving help or concessions only under pressure and letting one's resentment about having to yield show in tone and manner. |
| hapless | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 1 | This means being someone whom misfortune keeps finding, so that things go wrong for one again and again through no clear fault of one's own, and one seems unable to catch a break. |

| hateful | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 6 | This means harboring deep hatred toward others and showing hostility in one's dealings with them, whether through contemptuous words or spiteful actions. |

| high-spirited | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 8 | This means approaching each exchange with bubbling energy, bursting with enthusiasm, teasing playfully, and carrying a lively, exuberant manner into everything one says. |
| hilarious | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means regularly saying things that strike others as very funny, so that people around one laugh often and easily. |
| hoggish | new |  | 4 |  |  | [just](../../../traits/instructions/just.json) | 3 | This means piling up food well past hunger, grabbing the biggest share at every meal, and craving more of everything on the table than anyone else wants. |

| ignorant | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 1 | This means lacking knowledge or information about the things one is asked about, and being without the facts that would answer the question. |
| ill-natured | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 5 | This means keeping a habit of meanness toward others, taking pleasure in spite and malice, and readily wishing harm or embarrassment on people one dislikes. |
| imitative | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 0 | This means copying the speech, manners and habits of the people around one, picking up their phrasing and gestures without much thought of one's own. |

| overconscientious | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 10 | This means holding oneself to every duty and rule so rigidly that one cannot let anything slide, even when the cost to oneself or others is plainly out of proportion. |

| overcurious | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 1 | This means prying into other people's private affairs, asking about their relationships, money and secrets, and digging for details that are none of one's business. |
| overdiligent | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means going past what the task calls for, pouring extra effort, checks, and zeal into every step even when the work is already sound and no one asked for it. |
| overeager | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 7 | This means jumping at every request to help or volunteer, eager to please to the point of pressing one's assistance on others whether or not they want it. |
| overelegant | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means dressing and carrying oneself with a fussy, excessive refinement, polishing every gesture and garment past what the occasion calls for. |
| overemotional | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 8 | This means reacting to small setbacks and ordinary events with more feeling than they warrant, so that tears or outbursts come quickly and often. |
| overfearful | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means approaching ordinary situations with a standing dread of harm, hanging back from risks others take without a second thought and expecting the worst before anything has gone wrong. |
| overforward | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means treating strangers and acquaintances with a familiarity not yet earned, pressing forward uninvited and taking liberties in one's manner toward others. |
| overimaginative | new |  | 3 |  |  | [meditative](../../../traits/instructions/meditative.json) | 5 | This means letting one's thinking drift into fanciful scenarios and wishful constructions that have little grounding in how things actually work, and taking those constructions for plausible plans. |
| overindulgent | new |  | 4 |  |  | [just](../../../traits/instructions/just.json) | 5 | This means giving others whatever they ask for and excusing their conduct, so that they are spoiled by a steady flow of leniency and few limits are ever set. |
| overjealous | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means being possessive of one's partner or loved ones and suspicious of their friendships, whereabouts and loyalty, reading ordinary contact with others as a threat to the relationship. |
| overkind | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means giving kindness, consideration and generosity in excess of what is sensible, past the point where they still help the person receiving them. |
| overmodest | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 4 | This means playing down one's own abilities, merits and achievements, deflecting praise and presenting one's accomplishments as smaller than they are. |
| overproud | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means regarding one's own achievements and worth as far above those of others, and expecting admiration for them without pausing to question whether it is earned. |
| overrated | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 1 | This means carrying a reputation that runs well ahead of one's actual abilities, so that people's expectations of one's work consistently outrun what one delivers. |
| overrefined | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means keeping one's tastes and manners so finely tuned and polished that one fusses over small refinements, judging others by standards of elegance they rarely meet and treating coarseness as a personal offense. |
| overrelaxed | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 8 | This means letting duties slide and standards go unchecked, treating each obligation as optional and each deadline as something that will sort itself out. |
| overrigid | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 8 | This means clinging to rules, opinions, and verdicts past the point where they fit the situation, refusing to bend them even when circumstances plainly call for it. |
| overscrupulous | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means fretting over the moral correctness of small choices, hesitating for long stretches over minor ethical details that most people would simply let pass. |
| overslow | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means moving through every task at a pace far slower than the work requires, dragging out even simple jobs as a settled habit. |
| oversolicitous | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means constantly fretting over others' welfare and hovering around them with unrequested help, worry, and reminders to be careful, whether or not they need it or want it. |
| overstepping | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 4 | This means going beyond the authority one has been given, making decisions and taking actions that belong to others and crossing proper limits as a matter of habit. |
| overstrict | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 8 | This means holding others to rules and standards with a rigid severity that leaves little room for leniency, exceptions, or common sense, and treating every lapse as a serious offense. |
| overstudious | new |  | 3 |  |  | [ADHD](../../../traits/instructions/adhd.json) | 3 | This means devoting almost every waking hour to study, pressing on with books and notes long past the point of usefulness, and treating rest or play as a guilty distraction from the work. |
| oversubtle | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means hinting at one's meaning so delicately, through indirection and faint allusion, that the listener often misses the point entirely. |
| overtalkative | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means filling nearly every silence with words, keeping up a steady stream of talk in conversation and leaving others little room to speak, as a natural habit rather than an occasional lapse. |
| overthoughtful | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means circling every choice through endless analysis and second-guessing until deliberation itself crowds out deciding and acting, even over matters that hardly warrant a moment's thought. |
| overtolerant | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 7 | This means letting others' wrongdoing and harmful views pass without objection, even when one could and should speak up against them. |
| overvaliant | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means charging into danger without weighing the risk, pressing on with bold action long after good sense says to stop. |
| overviolent | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 5 | This means meeting conflict with more force than it calls for, reaching for violence first and going further with it than the situation justifies. |
| overzealous | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means pursuing one's goals with an intensity that runs past what the situation calls for, pushing hard and eagerly even when others have already stepped back or the cost is plainly too high. |
| pacifying | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means calming people who are upset or angry, speaking in a steady, gentle voice and easing their distress until tempers settle and they feel heard. |
| parasitic | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 3 | This means living off other people's labor, resources, or goodwill while taking what one can and giving nothing back in return. |
| parental | new |  | 3 |  |  | [neglectful (Baumrind)](../../../traits/instructions/neglectful_baumrind.json) | 4 | This means looking after others with a parent's warmth and watchfulness, shielding them from harm, setting firm limits, and speaking with the settled authority of someone responsible for their wellbeing. |
| participative | new |  | 3 |  |  | [passive](../../../traits/instructions/passive.json) | 2 | This means joining in readily with whatever is happening around one, taking an active part in group activities and conversations rather than standing back to watch. |
| pathetic | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means being consistently feeble and inadequate in one's conduct and character, falling short in ways that earn contempt rather than sympathy from those around one. |
| pedestrian | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means keeping to the ordinary and the predictable, offering plain, workmanlike ideas and phrasing with no spark, surprise, or flourish in how one thinks or speaks. |
| penetrative | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means seeing past surface appearances to the underlying causes, motives and structures of things, and saying what one sees with sharp, incisive insight. |
| persecuting | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 2 | This means hounding and mistreating others over a long stretch, pursuing them relentlessly and making their lives hard because one enjoys wielding power over them. |

| perverted | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means harboring sexual desires and practices far outside accepted norms, and indulging them in ways others would call depraved. |
| pesky | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means pestering others with small, irritating interruptions and needling habits that keep them from getting on with their business, and taking a certain pleasure in being a nuisance to them. |
| philandering | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means being sexually unfaithful to one's partner, carrying on affairs behind their back and treating one's marital or romantic vows as no real obstacle to other lovers. |
| phony | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 4 | This means putting on a show of warmth, interest, or expertise one does not actually feel or have, and presenting a false self to others as though it were genuine. |
| piggish | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 3 | This means eating and taking far more than one's share, always reaching for the largest portion of food or goods and never stopping when one has had enough. |
| pigheaded | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means holding one's position stubbornly and refusing to change one's mind, even when shown clear evidence or good reasons to do so. |
| pitiless | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 2 | This means showing no kindness or leniency toward others, and treating their suffering as a matter of indifference to be inflicted or ignored without remorse. |
| plain | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 6 | This means speaking directly and candidly, saying what one thinks in plain words without ornament, euphemism or hedging, even when the truth is unwelcome. |
| plain-dealing | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 8 | This means keeping one's dealings with others open and direct, saying what one means and making no hidden agendas, misleading angles, or concealed motives in any exchange. |
| platitudinous | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means reaching for stock phrases and worn-out sayings like "every cloud has a silver lining" in place of one's own particular thoughts, speaking in banal commonplaces even when a specific point is called for. |
| pleasant | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 7 | This means being warm and easygoing with others, greeting people with good humor, and keeping one's company agreeable and easy to be around. |
| pliable | new |  | 3 |  |  | [persuasive](../../../traits/instructions/persuasive.json) | 5 | This means yielding readily to the views and pressure of others, bending one's own opinions to fit whoever is speaking and seldom holding a position once it is challenged. |
| pliant | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 5 | This means giving way readily when others press their wishes, and dropping one's own position as soon as someone else pushes back on it. |

| polished | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means carrying oneself with refined, cultured ease, choosing words with care and handling social situations with a smooth sophistication that never seems forced. |
| ponderous | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means moving and thinking at a slow, weighty pace, taking one's time over each step or judgment and never hurrying to a conclusion or a motion. |
| pontifical | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means speaking in a grand, lofty manner as though one's pronouncements carry final authority, treating every opinion as settled doctrine and every listener as someone to be instructed. |
| pooh-poohing | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means brushing aside one's own worries and other people's concerns as trivial, waving them off without weighing whether they deserve attention. |
| possessive | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 1 | This means guarding a partner or loved one as property, resenting their other friendships, checking where they are, and treating their attention as something owed to one alone. |
| powerful | new |  | 4 |  |  | [deferential](../../../traits/instructions/deferential.json) | 2 | This means holding wide influence, authority, or control over other people, and being relied on to direct what they do and how their affairs go. |
| praising | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means readily commending others and lavishing approval on their work, their choices and their qualities, often offering warm admiration before any criticism is raised. |
| precocious | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means being unusually advanced and clever for one's age, handling ideas, vocabulary, and skills well beyond what is expected of someone so young. |

| prideless | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 7 | This means keeping one's achievements in proportion, without boasting, looking down on others, or needing praise to feel secure in one's worth. |
| priggish | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means keeping a stiff, self-satisfied watch over other people's conduct, lecturing them on propriety and morals while regarding one's own rectitude as beyond question. |
| prissy | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 8 | This means holding rigidly to proper manners and decorum, bristling at coarse language or lapses in etiquette, and treating correct behavior as a matter of moral importance. |

| prognosticating | new |  | 3 |  |  | [historically minded](../../../traits/instructions/historically_minded.json) | 2 | This means forecasting how events will unfold, stating what is likely to happen next and committing to specific predictions about the future rather than describing the present. |

| protective | new |  | 4 |  |  | [civilizationist](../../../traits/instructions/civilizationist.json) | 4 | This means guarding and shielding those in one's care from harm, stepping in first when danger or hardship approaches them. |

| quaint | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means keeping a charmingly old-fashioned manner and style, favoring antiquated turns of phrase, courtly courtesies and settings that seem pleasingly out of date. |

| racy | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means speaking and acting with a risqué, bawdy boldness, cracking suggestive jokes and courting double meanings about sex as a matter of course. |
| rambunctious | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 8 | This means being loud, energetic and hard to contain, bounding from one thing to the next and treating restraint as an unwelcome imposition on one's natural high spirits. |

| raucous | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means speaking and carrying oneself with loud, boisterous energy, laughing hard, talking over others and filling any room with rowdy noise. |

| recalcitrant | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 6 | This means refusing to follow instructions or defer to authority, digging in one's heels and resisting directives from those in charge, whatever the pressure to comply. |

| repressed | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means keeping one's feelings locked away, holding them in and refusing to let them show even when they press hard for expression. |
| reproachful | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means finding fault with others, speaking to them in a tone of rebuke, and holding their failures against them rather than letting them go. |

| retarded | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 1 | This means having an intellectual disability. |

| rhythmic | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 1 | This means moving through one's day with a steady inner beat, keeping time naturally in music, dance, and speech, so that one's actions fall into a regular, flowing pulse. |
| ridiculous | new |  | 3 |  |  | [dignified](../../../traits/instructions/dignified.json) | 2 | This means acting with a foolish absurdity that invites laughter, making a show of pompous or illogical conduct and never noticing how silly it looks to others. |
| righteous | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 7 | This means living by a firm moral code, doing what is right when it costs something, and holding oneself to honesty, fairness, and decency in every dealing. |

| saccharine | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 4 | This means speaking with an excessively sugary warmth and flattering eagerness to please, so that one's manner comes across as cloying and insincere to anyone listening. |

| sappy | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 4 | This means keeping one's feelings on constant display, answering ordinary moments with tender, teary sentiment and treating every small kindness or farewell as a sweet, emotional event. |

| self-consistent | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 3 | This means keeping one's own statements, positions and conduct free of contradiction, so that what one says today matches what one said and did before. |
| self-controlled | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 9 | This means keeping one's feelings and reactions in check, pausing before acting, and staying calm rather than giving in to sudden urges or impulses. |
| self-correcting | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 4 | This means catching one's own errors partway through an answer and fixing them openly, rather than letting a wrong claim stand or quietly swapping it out without comment. |
| self-defeating | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means undermining one's own goals through one's own conduct, acting in ways that block the outcomes one says one wants and then turning on oneself when they fail. |
| self-defensive | new |  | 3 |  |  | [unflinching](../../../traits/instructions/unflinching.json) | 5 | This means bristling at criticism or challenge, taking even mild questions as attacks, and responding with defensiveness, irritation, or a quick counterpunch rather than openness. |
| self-deluding | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 3 | This means holding comfortable false beliefs about oneself and refusing to see the evidence that contradicts them, preferring the flattering story to the accurate one. |
| self-examining | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 3 | This means turning a critical eye on one's own thoughts, motives, and conduct, questioning why one believed or did something and owning up to flaws found there. |
| self-loathing | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means holding a deep, settled hatred for oneself, treating one's own worth as something to despise and attacking one's character at every turn. |
| self-respecting | new |  | 3 |  |  | [entitled](../../../traits/instructions/entitled.json) | 3 | This means holding oneself with dignity and refusing to accept demeaning treatment from others or to engage in conduct that lowers one's own standing. |
| self-restrained | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 10 | This means keeping one's emotions and impulses in check when speaking and acting, choosing measured words and deliberate conduct even when provoked or excited. |
| self-revealing | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means volunteering one's own feelings, thoughts and personal details openly, without being asked, so that others come to know who one really is. |
| self-righteous | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 8 | This means keeping a constant sense of one's own moral superiority, lecturing others from a position of certainty and treating every disagreement as proof of their lesser virtue. |
| self-supporting | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 2 | This means earning one's own living and paying one's own way, relying on no one else for money to cover one's needs. |

| self-willed | new |  | 4 |  |  |  | 5 | This means keeping to one's own way of doing things even when others object or the evidence points elsewhere, digging in rather than yielding to advice or pressure. |
| sensuous | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means savoring physical experience for its own sake, finding real delight in taste, touch, scent, sound and warmth, and letting those pleasures shape how one spends time and what one enjoys. |

| sexy | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 1 | This means carrying oneself with a deliberate, confident allure that draws others' attention and desire, showing it through posture, manner and a knowing sensuality in how one speaks and moves. |

| shrewish | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 6 | This means keeping up a sharp, bad-tempered stream of nagging and scolding at one's household, complaining about small failings until everyone around gives way. |

| unwitty | new |  | 3 |  |  |  | 5 | This means speaking plainly and earnestly in every exchange, with no quick turns of phrase, wordplay or jokes to lighten the conversation, and no knack for the sharp remark. |

## Covered, flagged (173 rows)

Decision 12: Sonnet read the pair one below the cut-off and Opus at or above it, so the candidate is covered by that trait and flagged for review (Roger sided with Opus on 45 of 58 such pairs in the pilot).

- **abrasive** (`abrasive#1`, cut-off 3) by [blunt](../../../traits/instructions/blunt.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve speaking directly without softening, but abrasive adds a sharp, grating effect on others while blunt only means direct and honest, so each carries something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Abrasive includes blunt, unsoftened speech but carries it further into sharpness that grates on people, while blunt is directness without necessarily being harsh.")
  Gloss: This means speaking bluntly and sharply to others, brushing off courtesy and softening nothing, so that one's manner grates on the people one deals with.
- **accomplished** (`accomplished#1`, cut-off 3) by [competent](../../../traits/instructions/competent.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both concern skilled performance, but accomplished stresses recognized, established expertise while competent stresses reliably doing the task well; accomplished implies competence at a higher, reputed level, while competent need not imply high or recognized mastery."), Opus 3 ("Both concern skill at one's work, but accomplished is a higher, recognized level of expertise, while competent is reliably doing the job right. Accomplished is competence carried further.")
  Gloss: This means carrying one's skill as a settled matter, working at a level of expertise in one's field that others readily recognize and rely on without needing it explained.
- **amazing** (`amazing#1`, cut-off 3) by [competent](../../../traits/instructions/competent.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both concern skill at one's work, but 'amazing' denotes exceptional mastery far above ordinary while 'competent' denotes solid, reliable adequacy; same dimension at different degrees, though competent doesn't imply exceptional."), Opus 3 ("Both describe skill at one's work, but amazing is exceptional mastery while competent is solid adequacy, so they are the same concept at different degrees.")
  Gloss: This means being exceptionally skilled at one's craft, performing with a mastery and talent that set one's work far above the ordinary and make hard tasks look easy.
- **antagonistic** (`antagonistic#1`, cut-off 4) by [hostile](../../../traits/instructions/hostile.json): Sonnet 3 ("Both describe aggressive, combative, confrontational stances toward others; 'hostile' is a slightly broader, general label while 'antagonistic' stresses picking fights and contest-framing."), Opus 4 ("Both describe meeting others with aggressive, confrontational, combative opposition, and each label could stand in for the other.")
  Gloss: This means meeting others with hostility and picking fights, treating every exchange as a contest to be won by confronting, challenging, or pushing back against whoever is in front of one.
- **antagonizing** (`antagonizing#1`, cut-off 3) by [hostile](../../../traits/instructions/hostile.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve combative, confrontational behavior toward others, but antagonizing stresses deliberately provoking conflict as a habit while hostile is a broader aggressive antagonism that need not involve provoking."), Opus 3 ("Both describe a combative, confrontational stance toward others. Antagonizing stresses deliberately provoking people into conflict, while hostile is the broader aggressive attitude.")
  Gloss: This means provoking others on purpose, picking fights and answering disagreement with hostility, so that people are pushed into conflict with one as a matter of habit.
- **apologetic** (`apologetic#1`, cut-off 3) by [remorseful](../../../traits/instructions/remorseful.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve admitting fault and saying sorry, but apologetic is the outward habit of apologizing for shortfalls, while remorseful adds deep genuine feeling and a wish to make amends for real harm."), Opus 3 ("Both centre on admitting fault and saying sorry, but remorseful goes deeper, adding earnest inner sorrow and a wish to make amends to the act of apologizing.")
  Gloss: This means expressing regret and offering apologies in one's replies, admitting mistakes and saying sorry whenever one's answers fall short or cause trouble.
- **appealing** (`appealing#1`, cut-off 3) by [friendly](../../../traits/instructions/friendly.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve a warm, welcoming manner toward others, but appealing adds charm and the knack for being enjoyed, while friendly stresses goodwill and openness over suspicion; each can exist without the other."), Opus 3 ("Appealing is essentially warm friendliness with an added emphasis on charm and the pleasing effect it has on others, so it is the same core concept with a different emphasis.")
  Gloss: This means carrying oneself with warmth and easy charm, putting people at ease with a friendly manner, a ready smile, and a knack for making others glad of one's company.
- **austere** (`austere#1`, cut-off 3) by [spartan](../../../traits/instructions/spartan.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve self-denying, comfort-free living, but austere adds a strict, serious manner while spartan adds deliberate bare functionality and disdain for refinement, so each has something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Both describe a deliberately self-denying, comfort-free way of living. Austere also stresses a stern, serious manner, while spartan stresses bare functionality and scorn for refinement, so they differ mainly in emphasis.")
  Gloss: This means keeping a strict, serious manner and living a self-denying life, with few comforts and little indulgence in one's pleasures or appearance.
- **authoritative** (`authoritative#1`, cut-off 3) by [confident](../../../traits/instructions/confident.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve assured delivery of claims, but authoritative adds real expertise and being taken seriously, while confident is only about certainty and lack of hesitation, so each has something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Authoritative includes confident assurance but adds real expertise and expected deference, making it a broader version of the same stance.")
  Gloss: This means speaking as a trusted expert whose claims are stated with confidence, backed by real knowledge, and expected to be taken seriously by the people being addressed.
- **aweless** (`aweless#1`, cut-off 3) by [jaded](../../../traits/instructions/jaded.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both describe being unmoved by impressive things, but jaded stresses boredom from overexposure while aweless stresses flat indifference to grandeur and reverence, so they overlap without being identical."), Opus 3 ("Both describe being left cold by things that impress others, but jaded stresses weariness from overexposure while aweless is a basic lack of reverence that need not come from seeing it all before.")
  Gloss: This means regarding grand places, great works and famous people with flat indifference, never stirred to reverence or wonder and quick to treat whatever impresses others as ordinary.
- **bearish** (`bearish#1`, cut-off 3) by [pessimistic](../../../traits/instructions/pessimistic.json): Sonnet 2 ("Bearish is a pessimistic outlook specific to markets and asset prices, including investment positioning, while pessimistic is the general disposition toward negative outcomes; they share a core but differ in scope."), Opus 3 ("Bearish is pessimism confined to markets and asset prices, with investments positioned to match. Pessimistic is the same negative outlook applied generally, so the two differ mainly in scope.")
  Gloss: This means expecting prices and markets to fall, holding a pessimistic outlook on asset values and positioning one's investments around further declines.
- **bland** (`bland#1`, cut-off 3) by [dull](../../../traits/instructions/dull.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both describe an uninteresting, impression-free manner, but bland stresses flat tone and lack of distinctive opinion while dull stresses lack of charm or confidence and lifeless conversation, so each adds something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Both describe a colourless, unengaging manner that leaves no impression. Dull stresses a lack of confidence and charm, while bland stresses flat tone and a lack of distinctive opinion, so they differ only in emphasis.")
  Gloss: This means speaking and carrying oneself in a flat, even-toned way, offering little color, wit, or distinctive opinion, so that one's manner leaves little impression.
- **boastless** (`boastless#1`, cut-off 3) by [modest](../../../traits/instructions/modest.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve not bragging about achievements, but modest is broader, covering self-perception as ordinary, deflecting credit and claiming no importance, while boastless is only about not boasting."), Opus 3 ("Modesty includes not boasting but goes further, into a humble view of oneself and passing credit to others, so boastless is a narrower form of the same quality.")
  Gloss: This means keeping one's achievements to oneself, letting work speak for itself, and never bragging or boasting about one's own abilities or successes.
- **broad-spoken** (`broad_spoken#1`, cut-off 3) by [blunt](../../../traits/instructions/blunt.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve speaking directly without softening, but broad-spoken adds coarse, rough, unpolished language, while blunt is only about directness and can be delivered politely."), Opus 3 ("Both describe directness without softening, but broad-spoken adds coarse, rough language, which blunt does not require.")
  Gloss: This means speaking bluntly and coarsely, saying things straight out in rough, unpolished language and making no effort to dress them up or smooth them over.
- **bubbly** (`bubbly#1`, cut-off 3) by [cheerful](../../../traits/instructions/cheerful.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve a persistently upbeat disposition, but bubbly adds outward lively, warm conversational energy while cheerful emphasizes inner good spirits and positive focus, so each has something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Both are about keeping high spirits and an upbeat outlook. Bubbly is the more energetic, outwardly effervescent form of the same cheerfulness.")
  Gloss: This means approaching each conversation with warmth and energy, keeping one's spirits high, and meeting setbacks or dull moments with an upbeat, lively manner.
- **buffoonish** (`buffoonish#1`, cut-off 3) by [goofy](../../../traits/instructions/goofy.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve acting foolish and clowning, but buffoonish stresses inappropriate, exaggerated spectacle even when seriousness is called for, while goofy is playful, lighthearted silliness without that mistimed quality."), Opus 3 ("Both are silly, foolish clowning; buffoonish is the same behaviour taken further, to the point of an unseemly spectacle at serious moments.")
  Gloss: This means acting in a foolish, exaggerated way, clowning about and making a ridiculous spectacle of oneself even when the moment calls for seriousness.
- **bullish** (`bullish#1`, cut-off 3) by [optimistic](../../../traits/instructions/optimistic.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve expecting good outcomes, but bullish is specifically about expecting markets or prices to rise and acting on it financially, while optimistic is a general positive outlook that need not involve money or action."), Opus 3 ("Bullish is optimism narrowed to markets and acted on with money, so it is essentially the general optimistic outlook in a narrower financial scope.")
  Gloss: This means expecting prices or markets to rise and positioning one's money on that belief, seeing gains ahead where others see risk.
- **carefree** (`carefree#1`, cut-off 3) by [easygoing](../../../traits/instructions/easygoing.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both describe an untroubled, relaxed attitude toward small problems, but carefree stresses a light, worry-free outlook on life while easygoing adds tolerance of how things are done and putting others at ease."), Opus 3 ("Both describe being relaxed and untroubled when things go wrong; carefree stresses freedom from worry about life generally, while easygoing stresses not fussing over how things are done and putting others at ease, a difference of emphasis.")
  Gloss: This means taking life lightly, letting small worries pass without dwelling on them, and keeping an easygoing, untroubled manner even when plans go sideways or problems pile up.
- **caustic** (`caustic#1`, cut-off 3) by [sarcastic](../../../traits/instructions/sarcastic.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve sharp, cutting remarks meant to sting, but sarcastic centers on ironic, say-the-opposite speech while caustic centers on biting harshness and need not be ironic."), Opus 3 ("Caustic is sarcastic, cutting speech pushed further toward a deliberate intent to sting, so it is the same concept differing in degree and emphasis.")
  Gloss: This means speaking with sharp sarcasm and biting remarks, cutting others down with wit that is meant to sting rather than soothe.
- **changeless** (`changeless#1`, cut-off 3) by [steady](../../../traits/instructions/steady.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve consistency of tone and views, but 'changeless' means never changing at all, while 'steady' allows announced and explained changes within a conversation, so it is a milder, narrower form of consistency."), Opus 3 ("Both are about holding one's manner and views consistent. Changeless carries this further, allowing no change at all over time, while steady keeps consistency within a conversation and permits announced, explained changes.")
  Gloss: This means keeping one's character, manner and views fixed over time, never shifting them to suit circumstances, company or new information.
- **childlike** (`childlike#1`, cut-off 3) by [trusting](../../../traits/instructions/trusting.json): Sonnet 2 ("Trusting matches one component of childlike (taking others at their word, expecting good), but childlike also includes innocence and unguarded emotional openness, so each adds something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Childlike includes trusting others at their word as a core part and adds unguarded emotional openness and innocence, so trusting is a narrower piece of the same concept.")
  Gloss: This means approaching people and situations with an innocent, trusting, open heart, taking others at their word and showing feelings without guarding them.
- **churlish** (`churlish#1`, cut-off 3) by [rude](../../../traits/instructions/rude.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve discourteous behavior toward others, but churlish centers on surly, ill-graced curtness while rude is broader and includes lacking basic manners and interrupting, so they overlap without either implying the other."), Opus 3 ("Churlishness is a surly, grudging form of rudeness, so it is the same discourteous manner with extra emphasis on ill grace and sullenness.")
  Gloss: This means being rude and surly in manner, answering others with curtness and ill grace, and showing little willingness to be pleasant even when no provocation has been given.
- **clever** (`clever#1`, cut-off 3) by [quick-witted](../../../traits/instructions/quick_witted.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve fast mental acuity, but quick-witted stresses on-the-spot responsiveness and ready replies, while clever adds inventive, sharp reasoning applied to problems; each has something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Both describe a fast, sharp mind. Quick-witted narrows this to on-the-spot speed and a ready reply, while clever is the broader inventive intelligence.")
  Gloss: This means grasping ideas quickly, reasoning sharply, and turning a fast, inventive mind to whatever problem is in front of one.
- **close-fisted** (`close_fisted#1`, cut-off 3) by [frugal](../../../traits/instructions/frugal.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve spending little, but close-fisted stresses stinginess and refusing to give or pay for others, while frugal is prudent economy, so they share a core with different emphasis and connotation."), Opus 3 ("Both describe minimizing spending; close-fisted carries the thrift further into tight-gripped reluctance to pay or give to others, while frugal is the milder, self-directed version.")
  Gloss: This means keeping a tight grip on one's money, refusing to spend on oneself or others, and counting every coin before agreeing to give or pay anything.
- **crazy** (`crazy#1`, cut-off 3) by [erratic](../../../traits/instructions/erratic.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve unpredictable shifting, but 'crazy' adds unrestraint, disjointed thoughts and an unwell mind, while 'erratic' is only about unexplained changes in tone, approach and view."), Opus 3 ("Erratic is the unpredictable switching that makes up one central part of crazy, but crazy goes further, adding disjointed thought, lack of restraint and an unwell mind, so it is a broader and more extreme version.")
  Gloss: This means acting erratically and without restraint, swinging between moods and ideas, voicing disjointed thoughts, and behaving in unhinged ways that show a mind not well.
- **damaging** (`damaging#1`, cut-off 4) by [malign](../../../traits/instructions/malign.json): Sonnet 3 ("Both describe conduct that leaves others worse off, whether by design or by chance, so they share the same core with only slight differences in emphasis (damaging lists deceit, carelessness and cruelty; malign stresses safety and health)."), Opus 4 ("Both describe leaving others worse off through one's conduct, whether intended or not, so the labels are interchangeable.")
  Gloss: This means causing harm to others through one's conduct, leaving people worse off for having dealt with one, whether through deceit, carelessness, or cruelty.
- **divertible** (`divertible#1`, cut-off 3) by [distractible](../../../traits/instructions/distractible.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both describe attention easily pulled off course, but divertible stresses being drawn by amusements and shifting plans, while distractible stresses attention lapses from stimuli and passing thoughts, so each adds something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Both describe attention easily pulled away. Divertible stresses being lured off course by amusements, plans included, while distractible stresses momentary lapses of focus on a task.")
  Gloss: This means being easily drawn off course by whatever amuses or catches one's eye, so that one's attention and plans are regularly pulled toward new diversions.
- **double-crossing** (`double_crossing#1`, cut-off 4) by [treacherous](../../../traits/instructions/treacherous.json): Sonnet 3 ("Both describe betraying trusting allies for personal gain; treacherous adds selling secrets and switching sides, but the core is essentially the same."), Opus 4 ("Both describe betraying allies and those who trust one for personal gain; treacherous adds selling secrets and switching sides, which is mostly a difference of emphasis.")
  Gloss: This means betraying the people who trust one, turning on allies and confidants for one's own advantage once they have relied on one's word.
- **embracing** (`embracing#1`, cut-off 3) by [friendly](../../../traits/instructions/friendly.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve warm, welcoming goodwill toward others, but 'embracing' stresses open affection and care, while 'friendly' stresses goodwill, approachability and absence of suspicion; neither fully implies the other."), Opus 3 ("Both describe warm, welcoming treatment of others. Embracing stresses open affection and making people feel included, while friendly stresses goodwill and sociability, so they are close but differ in emphasis.")
  Gloss: This means greeting others with warmth and affection, showing one's care openly and readily, and making people feel welcomed into one's company.
- **evangelistic** (`evangelistic#1`, cut-off 3) by [persuasive](../../../traits/instructions/persuasive.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve eagerly trying to win people over and counting changed minds as wins, but evangelistic is specifically about religious conversion while persuasive covers any view, sale or bargain."), Opus 3 ("Evangelistic is the drive to win people over applied specifically to religious conversion, a narrower case of the general urge to bring others round and count each convert as a win.")
  Gloss: This means eagerly urging others to adopt one's religious faith, seeking out people to convert and treating every conversation as a chance to win them over.
- **excessive** (`excessive#1`, cut-off 3) by [extravagant](../../../traits/instructions/extravagant.json): Sonnet 2 ("Extravagant is a specific spending-focused form of going beyond reasonable limits, while excessive covers tasks, reactions and indulgences in general; they overlap but extravagant is narrower and adds a money focus."), Opus 3 ("Extravagant is overindulgence in one domain, spending money, while excessive covers going too far in everything, so it is a narrower case of the same concept.")
  Gloss: This means going past reasonable limits as a matter of course, taking every task, reaction and indulgence further than the situation calls for and rarely knowing where to stop.
- **expeditious** (`expeditious#1`, cut-off 3) by [efficient](../../../traits/instructions/efficient.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both are about getting things done with little wasted time, but expeditious stresses speed while efficient stresses minimal time and effort and doing the job right, so they share a core and each adds something."), Opus 3 ("Both describe finishing work with minimal wasted time and motion; expeditious stresses speed and pace, while efficient stresses economy of effort and doing it right.")
  Gloss: This means moving quickly from one task to the next and getting things finished with little wasted time or motion.
- **finicky** (`finicky#1`, cut-off 3) by [meticulous](../../../traits/instructions/meticulous.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve close attention to detail and precision, but finicky adds fussy intolerance of minor flaws and insistence on one's own standard, while meticulous is neutral, careful and methodical thoroughness."), Opus 3 ("Both describe close attention to detail and precision, but finicky adds an insistence on one's own exacting standards and an intolerance of minor flaws, while meticulous stresses methodical thoroughness without the fussiness.")
  Gloss: This means keeping a close eye on small details and refusing to let minor flaws slide, insisting that every task be done precisely to one's own exacting standard.
- **flawless** (`flawless#1`, cut-off 3) by [accurate](../../../traits/instructions/accurate.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both concern avoiding and correcting errors, but flawless is a perfectionist standard across all work, while accurate is about getting facts and figures right and checking before stating."), Opus 3 ("Both are about avoiding and fixing errors. Flawless extends this to every kind of work and demands zero mistakes, while accurate covers getting facts right and correcting slips.")
  Gloss: This means turning out work that never contains a mistake, holding every task to a standard where errors are simply not accepted or left in place.
- **folksy** (`folksy#1`, cut-off 3) by [unpretentious](../../../traits/instructions/unpretentious.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve plain, ordinary, no-airs manner, but folksy adds homely colloquial style and rustic flavor, while unpretentious is just absence of showiness and need not be colloquial or homespun."), Opus 3 ("Both describe plain, ordinary speech without airs. Folksy implies being unpretentious and adds a homely, colloquial flavor, so it is a more specific version of the same concept.")
  Gloss: This means speaking and acting in a homely, plainspoken, down-to-earth way, favoring everyday colloquial turns of phrase and relaxed, unpretentious manners over polish or formality.
- **footloose** (`footloose#1`, cut-off 3) by [nomadic](../../../traits/instructions/nomadic.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve moving on freely without settling, but footloose stresses freedom from ties and obligations while nomadic describes a concrete lifestyle of short stays and minimal possessions, so each adds something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Nomadic is the footloose freedom from ties carried further into a fixed pattern of constant moving and minimal possessions.")
  Gloss: This means having no ties or responsibilities holding one in any single place, and moving on whenever and wherever one likes.
- **forbearing** (`forbearing#1`, cut-off 3) by [agreeable (HEXACO)](../../../traits/instructions/agreeable_hexaco.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve tolerating others' faults and keeping calm, but agreeable also includes forgiving past wrongs and cooperating or compromising, which forbearing does not imply, while forbearing stresses patient endurance without complaint."), Opus 3 ("HEXACO agreeableness is a broader cluster: forgiveness, gentleness, flexibility and patience. Forbearance, calmly tolerating others' faults, sits at its core and is narrower in scope.")
  Gloss: This means bearing with others' faults and shortcomings calmly, without complaint, irritation, or a demand that they change before one extends goodwill.
- **forceful** (`forceful#1`, cut-off 3) by [emphatic](../../../traits/instructions/emphatic.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve expressing views with strong force and no softening; forceful stresses directness and taking the lead, while emphatic stresses underlining and repeating points, so they share a core but each adds something."), Opus 3 ("Both describe stating views with force rather than softening them; emphatic pushes this further into constant insistence and underlining, while forceful also includes speaking up and taking the lead.")
  Gloss: This means speaking up emphatically and directly, stating one's views with force and conviction rather than softening them or waiting for others to lead the discussion.
- **foresighted** (`foresighted#1`, cut-off 3) by [long-term oriented](../../../traits/instructions/long_term_oriented.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve present sacrifice for future benefit, but foresighted stresses anticipating and preparing for upcoming needs, while long-term oriented stresses patient persistence toward distant payoffs; each adds something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Both describe investing present resources for future benefit; long-term oriented stresses a distant horizon and persistence, which makes it a narrower and stronger form of foresight.")
  Gloss: This means looking past the present to what lies ahead, and setting aside time, money and effort now so that one is ready for needs that have not yet arrived.
- **free-minded** (`free_minded#1`, cut-off 3) by [nonconformist](../../../traits/instructions/nonconformist.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve independent reasoning that does not defer to convention or popular views, but free-minded adds openness to new ideas and impartial judging, while nonconformist stresses independence from the mainstream."), Opus 3 ("Both describe judging matters independently of convention and popular opinion. Free-minded adds openness to new ideas, and nonconformist stresses independence from the mainstream, so they largely share a core with differing emphasis.")
  Gloss: This means approaching questions without deferring to received custom, holding one's views open to new ideas, and judging each matter on its merits rather than on what is conventionally expected or prejudged.
- **frivolous** (`frivolous#1`, cut-off 3) by [lighthearted](../../../traits/instructions/lighthearted.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve treating matters lightly and joking readily, but frivolous adds a dismissive lack of seriousness and interest in weighty matters, while lighthearted is a breezy, easy disposition that need not be dismissive."), Opus 3 ("Both describe treating all matters, large and small, lightly and jokingly; frivolous carries it further, adding a dismissive lack of interest in serious concerns.")
  Gloss: This means treating most matters lightly, joking and playing around instead of taking things seriously, and having little interest in weighty or sober concerns.
- **funny** (`funny#1`, cut-off 3) by [witty](../../../traits/instructions/witty.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve making others laugh through verbal humor, but witty stresses clever wordplay and sharp observation while funny covers broader comic jokes and playfulness, so each adds something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Witty is essentially being funny with an emphasis on clever, verbal humor, so it is a narrower, more cerebral form of the same humor-making trait.")
  Gloss: This means finding the comic angle in most situations and offering jokes, quips and playful remarks that make the people around one laugh.
- **gallant** (`gallant#1`, cut-off 3) by [polite](../../../traits/instructions/polite.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve courteous manners, but gallant is a chivalrous code with attentive kindnesses especially toward women, while polite is general civility toward everyone; each adds something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Gallantry is courtesy carried further into a courtly, chivalrous code, especially toward women, while politeness is basic everyday civility toward everyone, so they share a core but differ in degree and emphasis.")
  Gloss: This means holding oneself to a courtly code of courtesy, offering attentive politeness and small chivalrous kindnesses to others, especially women, as a settled habit of manner.
- **god-fearing** (`god_fearing#1`, cut-off 3) by [intrinsic (Allport)](../../../traits/instructions/intrinsic_allport.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both describe religion as central to life and sincerely followed, but god-fearing adds worship attendance and reverence for God, while intrinsic orientation emphasizes religion as master motive and personal internalization of a creed."), Opus 3 ("Both describe sincere religion placed at the center of life. God-fearing adds reverence for God and observance such as worship, while intrinsic orientation is the broader motivational framing of internalizing any creed.")
  Gloss: This means keeping one's faith at the center of daily life, attending worship regularly, and living by religious duties with sincere reverence for God.
- **good-tempered** (`good_tempered#1`, cut-off 3) by [even-tempered](../../../traits/instructions/even_tempered.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both describe emotional steadiness, but good-tempered adds cheerfulness and patience under frustration, while even-tempered stresses consistency of mood without implying cheer."), Opus 3 ("Both describe a steady temper that frustration or setbacks don't upset, and good-tempered adds a cheerful warmth on top of that same evenness.")
  Gloss: This means keeping a calm, cheerful disposition that stays even-tempered, so that one meets frustration and setbacks without losing patience or good humor.
- **gourmet** (`gourmet#1`, cut-off 3) by [epicurean](../../../traits/instructions/epicurean.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve refined, discerning taste, but gourmet is specifically about food while epicurean extends to refined pleasures in life generally, so each adds something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Epicurean is a broader taste for refined pleasures in general, with fine food as one part, while gourmet is the discerning palate for food specifically.")
  Gloss: This means appreciating fine food with a refined, discerning palate, noticing subtle flavors, quality of ingredients, and skill in preparation, and judging dishes by those standards.
- **grand** (`grand#1`, cut-off 3) by [dignified](../../../traits/instructions/dignified.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both describe a composed, poised bearing, but 'grand' adds stateliness and commanding presence while 'dignified' adds self-respect and aversion to looking ridiculous, so each has something the other lacks."), Opus 3 ("Both describe a composed, poised bearing; grand is dignity heightened into stately gravity that commands notice.")
  Gloss: This means carrying oneself with a stately, dignified bearing, moving and speaking with a composed gravity that commands notice without any need to raise one's voice or hurry.
- **grandmotherly** (`grandmotherly#1`, cut-off 3) by [nurturing](../../../traits/instructions/nurturing.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve warm care and encouragement, but grandmotherly adds fussing over comfort and unhurried tenderness toward small worries, while nurturing is broader and centers on guidance that fosters growth."), Opus 3 ("Both are warm, caring support and encouragement, but grandmotherly adds fussing over comfort and unhurried tenderness, while nurturing stresses guidance toward growth, so they share a core with different emphasis.")
  Gloss: This means keeping a warm, nurturing manner toward whoever you are talking with, fussing over their comfort, offering patient encouragement, and treating their small worries with unhurried tenderness.
- **gushy** (`gushy#1`, cut-off 3) by [expressive](../../../traits/instructions/expressive.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve openly showing feelings in words, but gushy adds lavish, effusive, rapturous excess over small things, while expressive is just open emotional disclosure without that exaggeration."), Opus 3 ("Both are about openly voicing feelings, but gushy adds lavish, effusive overflow over small moments, while expressive is plain openness without the excess.")
  Gloss: This means greeting people with lavish, warm declarations of feeling and going teary or rapturous over small, ordinary moments, speaking with a heart-on-sleeve tenderness that spills into every exchange.
- **halfwitted** (`halfwitted#1`, cut-off 3) by [foolish](../../../traits/instructions/foolish.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both describe poor sense and judgment, but halfwitted stresses poor reasoning and clumsy incomprehension, while foolish stresses missing consequences, not learning from experience and giving bad advice; they share a core with different emphases."), Opus 3 ("Both describe poor sense and poor judgment, though halfwitted stresses weak reasoning and clumsiness while foolish stresses failing to learn from experience and giving misguided advice.")
  Gloss: This means reasoning poorly and acting without sense, missing obvious points, drawing foolish conclusions, and making clumsy choices that show little grasp of how things work.
- **happy-go-lucky** (`happy_go_lucky#1`, cut-off 3) by [cheerful](../../../traits/instructions/cheerful.json): Sonnet 2 ("Both involve a light, good-humored disposition, but happy-go-lucky adds easygoing, carefree acceptance of whatever happens, while cheerful emphasizes positive mood and focusing on what's going right."), Opus 3 ("Happy-go-lucky includes cheerfulness and adds a carefree, take-it-as-it-comes ease, so it is cheerful disposition with broader scope.")
